A look at how the Trumps spend their Thanksgiving holiday through the years

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Melania Trump delivers a powerful message of gratitude to military families during a visit to Fort Rucker ahead of Thanksgiving. She honors the profound sacrifices made by military spouses and children while their loved ones are deployed across the globe. She expresses deep appreciation for their strength and resilience in keeping their homes full of love.

In keeping with tradition, President Donald Trump marked his sixth Thanksgiving in the executive office with the White House’s annual Turkey Pardon ceremony on Tuesday.

His plans for Thanksgiving Day this year remain unannounced — but previous holidays offer a preview of how the president likes to spend the day honoring those who can't be at home for the uniquely American holiday due to service to the country.

Last year Trump spent Thanksgiving at Mar-a-Lago participating in his favorite pass time — golf. But he also used the time in what is known as the "Winter White House" to call Maj. Gen. Dave Gardner, commander of the 101st Airborne Division and Joint Task Force–Southern Border by video conference and talk with troops deployed along the southern border.

TRUMP AND DANA WHITE HONOR MARINE CORPS BAND WITH SIX-FIGURE CHECKS IN FIRST EVENT ON NEW HELIPAD

President Donald Trump delivers remarks to U.S. troops in an unannounced visit to Al Asad Air Base, Iraq, Dec. 26, 2018. (Reuters/Jonathan Ernst)

First lady Melania Trump spent the days ahead of the holiday in 2025 visiting Camp Lejeune and Marine Corps Air Station New River with Second Lady Usha Vance on Nov. 19. They met with military families and made Thanksgiving crafts with children.

In 2020, during the final Thanksgiving of his first term, Trump remained at the White House and made calls to six deployed military units representing every branch of the armed forces, including troops in Kuwait and sailors aboard the USSWinston Churchill in the Red Sea.

Trump famously took a secret trip to Bagram Airfield in Afghanistan on Nov. 28, 2019, where he served turkey, posed for photos and thanked American troops for their sacrifices amid the ongoing war in the Middle East, which not even two years later in August 2021 was brought to an end by then-President Joe Biden.

"There's nowhere I'd rather celebrate this Thanksgiving than right here with the toughest, strongest, best, and bravest warriors on the face of the Earth," Trump told the troops at the time.

"And you don't even know how much the people of our country love and respect you."

President Donald Trump and first lady Melania Trump greet military personnel at the dining facility during an unannounced visit to Al Asad Air Base, Iraq, Dec. 26, 2018. (Reuters/Jonathan Ernst)

TRUMP SAYS US MAY RECOVER ALL THE EQUIPMENT LEFT BEHIND IN AFGHANISTAN BY BIDEN ADMIN

The visit came as roughly 12,000 American troops remained in Afghanistan more than 18 years after the U.S. invasion.

During the visit, Trump met with Afghan President Ashraf Ghani to discuss efforts to end the war and told American troops that the Taliban wanted to reach a deal with the United States. He said his administration was reducing America's military presence in Afghanistan while pursuing a political solution to the more than 18-year conflict.

President Donald Trump visits with personnel at U.S. Coast Guard Station Lake Worth Inlet in Riviera Beach, Florida, on Thanksgiving Day, Nov. 22, 2018. (Mandel Ngan/AFP via Getty Images)

Trump spent Thanksgiving 2018 in Florida having a traditional dinner with his family, including Melania and his children and grandchildren.

He also held a video call with deployed troops before visiting Coast Guard members at Station Lake Worth Inlet with his wife, where the first couple thanked them for their service and hurricane rescues.

After that holiday in 2018, Melania and then-Second Lady Karen Pence assembled comfort kits for deployed troops at American Red Cross headquarters.

MOSCOW, TALIBAN FORGE MILITARY ALLIANCE IN POWER GRAB AFTER US AFGHANISTAN EXIT: REPORTS

President Donald Trump speaks to members of the military via teleconference from his Mar-a-Lago resort in Palm Beach, Florida, on Thanksgiving Day, Nov. 22, 2018. (Mandel Ngan/AFP via Getty Images)

Trump spent his first Thanksgiving as president at his Mar-a-Lago estate for a traditional Thanksgiving dinner with his family. It came after a day of video-calling deployed troops.

Donald and Melania Trump also visited Coast Guard Station Lake Worth Inlet in Riviera Beach, Florida, where they handed out food and greeted service members.

Ashley J. DiMella reports on politics for Fox News Digital.

Get the latest updates on the Trump administration and Congress, exclusive interviews and more Fox News politics content.

What SEOs Can Be Thankful For: 8 Wins To Celebrate In 2026 via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Where search leaders are putting 2027 budget, plus the AI visibility scorecard that replaces session reporting.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: State Of Agentic AEO For 2027: What’s Scaling & What’s Stalling

A Thanksgiving roundup of eight search changes from this year worth a thank-you, from AI visibility data and social reports to a back button policy.

In SEO, we like to complain, but this industry and craft is also about finding the small wins to celebrate. Today is a day to consider what we are thankful for, so let us dig deep and create our SEO Thanksgiving list.

Search may have given us plenty to navigate this year in AI, but Google, Microsoft, and OpenAI were each able to find their own ways to offer up some AI visibility. We can take the crumbs.

This article is the Search Engine Journal Thanksgiving list for all things SEO, social, local, and PPC. We finish off with a celebration for anybody who clicked their back button and went somewhere they had never been before.

Google Search Console is currently displaying dedicated reports for your impressions in AI Overviews, AI Mode, and generative AI features in Discover, by page, country, and date. These new reports were released in June, and Google’s announcement now says, “As of August 31, 2026, we’ve rolled out these insights to all websites worldwide.”

We’re glad to finally see the AI impressions separated out from the normal impression totals.

SEJ covered it here: Google Search Console AI Reports Rolled Out Worldwide

Microsoft Bing Webmaster Tools added an AI performance report in February as a public preview, showing how many times Microsoft Copilot, Bing’s AI summarizations, and some partner integrations reference your content, along with the referenced pages and the grounding query (the phrase used by the AI to retrieve the content).

In June, Microsoft added a Citation Share feature in preview, which provides insight into what percentage of citations for a grounding query go to your site. The February report gives you a count of how many times you appeared in the citation list, and Citation Share shows how large a piece of the pie those citations represent.

SEJ covered it here: Bing Webmaster Tools Adds AI Citation Performance Data and here Google Search Console AI Reports Rolled Out Worldwide

On Nov. 10, OpenAI launched ChatGPT Business Hub in beta. All eligible businesses where ChatGPT is available can claim their business in Business Hub and, after claims and identity verification are approved, will be able to see mentions, website clicks, and discovery topics over the past 28 days.

According to OpenAI’s setup guide for online businesses, a mention occurs when ChatGPT references your website, not if it just names your brand.

Vahan Petrosyan, director of technology for SEJ, submitted Search Engine Journal, and Business Hub showed 353,356 mentions and 2,663 website clicks from Oct. 14 through Nov. 10.

SEJ’s mentions and website clicks from Oct. 14 through Nov. 10. Screenshot by Vahan Petrosyan, Search Engine Journal.

This is a beta version, and OpenAI says that claiming a business does not assure greater visibility within ChatGPT. However, these numbers are provided directly from ChatGPT, and mentions cannot be reflected in referral reports.

SEJ covered it here: How to Submit Your Website to ChatGPT Business Hub

Google introduced platform properties for social and video accounts in July and made them available to everybody worldwide on July 29. They provide information regarding clicks, impressions, CTR and average position for posts on Instagram, TikTok, X and YouTube appearing in Google Search. Reports for Discover and Google News will also be displayed when applicable.

The Insights report for a YouTube platform property shows clicks from Google Search over 28 days. Source: Google Search Central (CC BY 4.0, cropped).

Your social team can now track how users locate the team’s social content on Google Search, within Search Console, the same tool utilized by your SEO team.

SEJ covered it here: Google Search Console Adds Reports For Social Posts

Google announced the branded queries filter last November and made it available to all eligible sites by March. The branded queries filter allows you to sort your Performance report queries into branded, including misspellings, and non-branded, so you can identify traffic coming from users searching specifically for your brand or products.

The Search Analytics API got the same branded split this month. Prior to this branded queries filter being implemented, tracking traffic based upon specific branded searches required “maintaining complex keyword regex lists,” as Google put it. Many will agree that it is refreshing to no longer need such lists.

SEJ covered it here: Branded Query Data Is Now In The Google Search Console API

Google’s September Small Business Bulletin described view counts for Business Profile posts as “currently rolling out globally.”

Once the rollout has completed for your Business Profile, each post card located under the dashboard section will indicate views generated from Search and Maps activity for posts from the past 18 months.

Google removed post metrics from Business Profiles in 2023. Now you can determine which posts receive the most views rather than making educated guesses. However, the bulletin said the counts were not accessible via the API yet.

SEJ covered it here: Google Adds Post View Counts To Business Profiles

For PPC advertisers working in TikTok Ads Manager, the Q4 product preview highlighted Impression Share, a common metric seen in Google Ads, along with Top 1 Impression Share for Search Ads campaigns.

Impression Share measures the percentage of eligible search results impressions that your ads capture. According to TikTok’s help section, availability of Impression Share may vary depending on account and market. Still, it can quickly reveal what portion of eligible search visibility you are leaving behind.

TikTok also added Keyword Packs (pre-made brand and category keywords) to the Keyword Suggestion Tool for a faster way to create keyword lists.

SEJ covered it here: TikTok Search Ads Adds Brand And Category Keyword Packs

Google began treating back button hijacking as a malicious practice in April and started enforcing this policy on June 15. Sites using back button hijacking could be subject to either a manual action or automated demotion.

On Nov. 23, Google released a new FAQ which says the policy will apply if the back button hijacking occurs in a user’s browser, including when they visit your page via social media. Although this policy cannot eliminate all cases of back button hijacking, websites currently utilizing this tactic may want to stop.

SEJ covered it here: Google Says Back Button Hijacking Policy Covers Social Visits

Our single request for next year’s Thanksgiving holiday is the ability to differentiate Search Console’s AI clicks from web clicks. Currently, every time someone clicks on an AI Overview or AI Mode link, that click counts in the same Performance report as clicks on your other search engine results.

Happy Thanksgiving from all the SEJ team.

Breaking news and insights to help you lead search strategies.

'Batman 1920' Design Revealed In Jaw-Dropping Concept Art

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A stunning Batman design is unveiled by an artist who makes the case for a new 1920s noir series starring a terrifying Dark Knight. Besides being one of the most popular heroes of all time in his traditional stories, Batman thrives in wildly different tones. Since Bruce Wayne lacks traditional superpowers, DC can place him anywhere from campy sci-fi romps, deadpan comedy, and Saturday-morning animated adventures to gritty and grounded 21st-century crime thrillers and psychological horror tales. The Dark Knight bends to match any creative vision, almost always to great success.

Batman's unmistakable design adapts just as effortlessly across genres and historical eras. All across DC’s multiverse and mainline continuity, artists have transformed his classic gray-and-black uniform into everything from Terry McGinnis' high-tech cyber-suit from Batman Beyond to the hulking, battle-scarred armor of The Dark Knight Returns and Absolute Batman's colossal working-class titan. DC has also reimagined Bruce Wayne as a feudal shinobi in Batman Ninja, a swashbuckling buccaneer in Leatherwing, a vampiric nightmare in Red Rain, a cosmic lantern-wielder in In Darkest Knight, and a medieval crusader in Dark Knights of Steel, all of which have put their own fresh spin on the Caped Crusader's iconic Batsuit.

Batman is a natural fit for dark environments and dark eras, which is why the idea of a Dark Knight who's active in historical periods like the nineteenth century or the early twentieth century is so popular with writers, artists, and fans.

Artist Santiago Latorre shares an extraordinary piece of concept art for a 1920s Batman created for his original comic project The Archivist. This Roaring Twenties Dark Knight wears a rugged Batsuit that feels genuinely forged from post-World War I surplus and early pulp terror, with a brownish-gray trench tunic cinched with a bulky leather utility belt and a large dark cape with a high collar. His cowl is terrifying, with long ears, glowing red goggles, and a red lower face that looks like an open mouth. This Batsuit evokes both the Shadow and Man-Bat, as well as Batman's original Detective Comics #27's debut suit.

Latorre's inspired fusion of Golden Age nostalgia and visceral creature-feature darkness fits the 1920s setting like a glove. Gotham criminals would genuinely mistake this red-scarved, glowing-eyed Batman for a supernatural urban legend rather than a man in a costume. An official DC Black Label miniseries or Elseworlds comic built around Latorre’s Prohibition-era Dark Knight could be a success, with a low-tech detective Batman confronting bootlegger mob bosses and dieselpunk reinventions of classic rogues in fog-drenched alleys lit by Art Deco streetlamps.

Santiago Latorre’s Batman 1920 design puts a thrilling, creature-inspired spin on an established superhero trend. DC practically invented this Elseworlds playground with Mike Mignola and Brian Augustyn’s Victorian masterpiece Gotham by Gaslight, before expanding into 1920s Lovecraftian horror in Batman: The Doom That Came to Gotham, 1940s gangster pulp in Batman: Nine Lives, and post-war retro-noir in Batman: Caped Crusader and Batman: The Brave and the Bold's Golden Age tributes. Across the aisle, Marvel tapped into that exact same pulpy appeal with Spider-Man Noir, who transformed Peter Parker into a trench-coat-wearing, goggle-eyed vigilante battling mobsters during the 1930s Great Depression.

Bruce Wayne isn’t the only Batman in the DC multiverse. Discover 45 versions of the Caped Crusader and which one is the most powerful of all.

What separates Latorre’s Roaring Twenties concept from those celebrated predecessors is how boldly it leans into monstrous pulp intimidation. While The Doom That Came to Gotham and Marvel's Spider-Man Noir dress their heroes in practical gear, Latorre fuses post-World War I trench attire with glowing crimson lenses, a blood-red scarf, and a Man-Bat-like cowl alongside the wide-winged 1939 chest symbol. The result honors classic retro-noir superheroes and makes Gotham’s Prohibition-era vigilante look like an authentic nocturnal demon ripped straight from a 1920s penny dreadful.

Would you like to see a new 1920s Batman series?

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

A Change of Tune: How the 2000s Changed How We Listen to Music in Cars

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

From the CD changer to the iPod and aux jack, the decade saw audio options shift—for better or for worse.

These days, each of us is in our own little world. We’ve forfeited a largely shared reality and replaced it with an almost infinite series of more private universes. And I’m going to be honest with you: Results have been mixed. The seeds of our collective solitude were planted in the decades before, and the worst of it wouldn’t hit us until the years after, but 2000–09 was the era when the monoculture really began to fracture and fragment into microcultures. The great American mainstream was dwindling in the Aughts, a narrative expressed through our music. New technology changed the way people across the world listened. And as Americans, we in particular experienced this historic shift through our car stereos.

This story originally appeared in Volume 38 of Road & Track.

Once we shook off Y2K anxiety, the new millennium began as normal. Fueled by boy bands, blockbuster hip-hop, and, somehow, Limp Bizkit, the recording industry was flush in 2000. What was big was huge, and whether you liked the songs or not, you knew them. My main musical memory of the year 2000 took place in a rental car, on a drive up to the mountains with my roommates. The mid-size sedan had a mere cassette player, so we made do with the radio. On that October afternoon, some station played the comeback single from a band that hadn’t released a studio album in three years: U2’s “Beautiful Day.” We turned it up, we nodded along, and we were impressed that a band could sound this good despite having members who were now 40. Best of all, we shared the experience with however many strangers were also tuned in to this frequency.

As the Aughts progressed, I found myself sliding into my car’s CD slot fewer store-bought discs and more homemade mixes I burned using my iBook after downloading music from iTunes. It was easy, it was cheap at 99 cents per song, and it was as frictionless as a click on the touchpad. I recently found a box of these discs in my garage, and each one is packed with music I have not thought about since I put them there. These early-­Aughts songs left me no physical album covers to remember them by; the playlists were not yet saved for posterity on streaming services. They vanished.

Then there was the iPod. Other MP3 players predated it, but the iPod was sleek and sexy and held up to 1000 songs, which I swear sounded like a lot in 2001. The forward-thinking among us might carry a special cord in our bag in case we found ourselves in a car with an auxiliary input. Traditional radio stations suffered as we ditched the DJs and did their job for free.

Satellite radio launched an effort to fill the void—XM in 2001, Sirius in 2002, then SiriusXM in 2008, with hundreds of hyperspecific-genre stations on the digital dial. Satellite radio broke us in to the concept of paying a monthly fee to listen to music we don’t own, and Spotify and Apple Music would run with that idea in the decades ahead. Now we all have access to virtually every piece of music ever recorded, but who is guiding us through it? It’s chaos. I find myself longing for those days before endless choices, before Bluetooth or aux cables, when I’d trust a stranger to play something for me while I was driving. When I’d be on a road trip and an unknown station would fuzz in and play a country song and a public service announcement, when a human being in a studio said something that streaked on radio waves across the desert air, and we were connected, until the station fuzzed out.

Of course, I can still do that. But the convenience culture that has continued to creep in since the Aughts has conditioned me to pull out my phone, wirelessly connect it to the car, and put on a mix or a podcast. I actually have to remind myself that those days were real, when the radio was once our go-to.

I miss those times. The new stuff from U2 is pretty good, though.

Thanksgiving night sky 2026 — What to look for after dinner tonight

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

From Saturn's magnificent rings to the waning gibbous moon, here's how to turn your Thanksgiving celebrations into a family skywatching adventure.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If your household is like most American families, then on Thursday you likely will all be sitting down with family and friends for a traditional Thanksgiving turkey dinner during the mid-to-late afternoon hours.

Then as darkness falls, some will probably migrate into the living room to catch some football or other holiday fare on television. But if you have a large gathering of family and friends at your home — and if your skies are mainly clear — why not invite everyone outside to gaze at the evening sky?

If you have binoculars, or better yet, a telescope, you just might end up turning this into a memorable family tradition of its own.

If I may paraphrase the mysterious voice from the 1989 movie, "Field of Dreams": "If you set up your telescope, they will come." And you'll get a big kick out of your Turkey Day guests uttering all the "oohs" and "ahs" or exclamations of "Wow!" with just one peek through the eyepiece.

As darkness falls, Saturn will glow at first magnitude, shining with a sedate, yellow-white light and appearing noticeably brighter than the star Fomalhaut, about 40 degrees (the equivalent of four fist-widths) to its lower right.

Its famous ring system is now tilted only about six degrees toward Earth but may be visible with steadied (or image-stabilized) high-power binoculars or through the eyepiece of a telescope magnifying over 30-power. The rings will seem to glow with an inner light and for those who have never had the pleasure of seeing them for themselves, that first view always elicits astonishment; kids especially most often will say: "Awesome!" or "No way!" I've even had people accuse me of using a slide of Saturn, to which I, of course, reply with, "No . . . that's the real thing."

Then, along about 6:25 p.m. local time, the waning gibbous moon, 2½ days past full phase and 91% illuminated, will begin to emerge from beyond the east-northeast horizon. Give it another hour and it will stand about 10 degrees above the horizon — equal to the width of your clenched fist held at arm's length. By then, the moon will be in a better position for evening study.

Even in binoculars, the moon is a striking sight to see. It's such a special object because it doesn't matter whether you live in a rural area or in the middle of a brightly lit city, for the moon can always be readily observed. It always looks spectacular whether you're using binoculars or a telescope. It never looks precisely the same no matter how often you view it and can be observed even on hazy or partially cloud-covered nights.

Pay particular attention to the line of darkness — called the terminator — features near that border stand out in bold relief; the shadows become stronger and details are more easily seen. Take note on this night of the circular dark area, bisected by the terminator: Mare Crisium — the "Sea of Crises." Immediately bordering above it is a 50-mile (80 km)-wide ring of very rugged mountains, rising as high as 14,400 feet (4,389 meters), which now resembles a huge crater partially in shadow when the sun is low.

Probably the one object that your Thanksgiving guests will want to see is the biggest and brightest of all the man-made objects orbiting the Earth: the International Space Station (ISS). Except for those living across the southern US, for most everywhere else, the ISS will be making visible passes on Thanksgiving evening.

Circling the Earth at an average altitude of 260 miles (420 km) and at a speed of 17,500 mph (28,200 kph), it can appear to move as fast as a high-flying jet airliner, sometimes taking as much as six or seven minutes to cross the sky. It can easily be confused with aircraft lights. Nominally, it appears white with a slight yellow tinge and at its very brightest it can sometimes appear to shine as bright as magnitude -5.6, which is twice as bright as the planet Venus! So, what is the viewing schedule on Thanksgiving for your hometown? You can easily find out by visiting one of three popular websites:

This site will tell you when and where to sight the ISS. All you need do is type in your city or town, then click on the map pinpoint to get all the details. You can even sign up to get email or text alerts when the space station is flying over.

This site will not only provide you with sighting information for the ISS, but also for many other satellites as well. You must first register and then you can input your location to generate a sighting schedule.

Like Heavens Above, you can get sighting information for the ISS and other satellites. Once you log on this site will automatically provide details based on your IP address ... or you can set a "custom" location.

Here at Space.com, we like to wish a very happy and safe Thanksgiving to all, and of course, clear skies!

Joe Rao serves as an instructor and guest lecturer at New York's Hayden Planetarium. He writes about astronomy for Natural History magazine, Sky and Telescope, The Old Farmer's Almanac and other publications.

He writes about astronomy for Natural History magazine, Sky & Telescope and other publications. You can find him on Twitter and YouTube tracking lunar and solar eclipses, meteor showers and more. To find out Joe's latest project, visit him on Twitter.

Live Science asked its readers what type of archaeological find they'd want to discover.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

From people's backyards to famous excavation sites, archaeological discoveries found the world over give clues about the cultures and lives of ancient peoples. For instance, the discovery of 1,200-year-old pennies in Denmark revealed that the Vikings melted down silver from the Muslim world, while an excavation of a 2,800-year-old tomb with sacrificed horses in Siberia revealed ties to the nomadic Scythians.

Other archaeological discoveries are important due to their historical significance or extraordinary preservation. For example, the 40,000-year-old "Lion Man" statuette was carved by humans in what is now Germany during the last ice age, and it may be the earliest known evidence of religious belief in the world.

Meanwhile, ancient texts can provide clues about mysterious and long-lost languages, such as the Linear A writing system from the ancient Minoans of Crete. Other texts, like the Rosetta stone, helped crack the code of Egyptian hieroglyphics, allowing researchers to better understand this ancient culture.

Each type of archaeological discovery unearths something new about the ancient world. This week, Live Science polled its readers on which of these types of findings they'd most like to uncover.

Over 290 readers weighed in, with 69% choosing that they'd like to discover an ancient text "that helps decipher a language."

The comments also reflected this trend, with one reader writing: "The interpretation of a newly deciphered language can lead to the discovery of other things like tombs and documents, as was the case with the Rosetta stone. Once Egyptologists could read hieroglyphs, they could discover other information which helped them in their research and digs."

Other readers highlighted the importance of finding ancient weapons, which 4% of participants voted for. As one commenter wrote: "War has always been responsible for where people gather and settle down together. Weapons therefore can reveal the ebb and flow of people across the countries, which for many years never existed. This therefore reveals a lot about the specific development in many different areas and ultimately perhaps as to why we actually live where we do."

In a similar vein, one commenter wished that they could "discover Attila's grave site, since no one has found [it] as of today."

Another reader reflected on the importance of finding ancient burial sites, writing: "I think that tombs are the most valuable finds in archaeology."

Do you agree or disagree with these readers? What would you most like to find? Let us know in the comments!

Kenna Hughes-Castleberry is the Content Manager at Live Science. Formerly, she was the Content Manager at Space.com and before that the Science Communicator at JILA, a physics research institute. Kenna is also a book author, with her upcoming book 'Octopus X' scheduled for release in spring of 2027. Her beats include physics, health, environmental science, technology, AI, animal intelligence, corvids, and cephalopods.

TSMC's A14 1.4nm-class node is ready for chip design

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

PDK v0.9 and ecosystem readiness enable A14 design-ins.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

TSMC announced at its 2026 Europe OIP Ecosystem Workshop this week that its A14 (1.4nm-class) fabrication technology is ready for customer design work and version 0.9 of its process design kit (PDK) is ready. TSMC also detailed Boost Cells, a way for the new node to deliver 14% higher performance compared to N2P, and how customers can migrate their designs to A13 in the future. Furthermore, Cadence and Synopsys have both completed full design-flow certification on an Arm Cortex-A725 CPU core, which gives chip designers two validated toolchains to build their 14A chips.

"A14 0.9 design ecosystem and the PDK are ready for design in," a TSMC spokesman said on stage at the 2026 Europe OIP Ecosystem Workshop.

A14 uses TSMC's 2nd Generation nanosheet transistors and its NanoFlex Pro standard cell architecture, which supports Boost Cells to enable higher performance as well as Low-Power Cells to reduce power consumption. Designers can place compact high-speed Boost Cells on critical paths (which resembles Intel's Turbo Cells supported by its 14A fabrication technology) and use Low-Power Cells with narrower nanosheets for general logic or parts of the chip that do not need high performance.

In general, TSMC promises A14 with NanoFlex Pro optimizations to deliver a 14% speed gain at the same power or a 23% reduction in total power at the same speed compared to N2P with NanoFlex. In a separate comparison against baseline N2P, TSMC put A14 at 50% higher energy efficiency, 22% higher drawn transistor density, and 23% higher logic density.

A14 also gives customers a path to A13. The foundry says customers can cut die area by about 6% without changes to their A14 design flow. Furthermore, to take even more advantage of A13 and get 5% higher energy efficiency, all the designers have to do is re-engineer the M0 metal layer, reshape OD, and use VT Flex cells (cells that can use different threshold-voltage options for their NMOS and PMOS transistors).

TSMC expects to start risk production using A14 in the second half of 2027, with mass production following in the second half of 2028. Chief executive C.C. Wei said in October that the company had already seen more customer activity on A14 than on N2 at a comparable stage of development and expects A14 to become a bigger node than its 2nm-class predecessor. A13 is set to reach the high-volume manufacturing stage in the second half of 2029.

One of the key parts of the TSMC announcement is the readiness of the 0.9 version of the PDK and the electronic design automation (EDA) tools ecosystem. What matters about v0.9 is that the industry considers this revision ready for design work, which means that customers can start designing chips with confidence that they will meet their performance and power goals. Furthermore, TSMC has validated two complete flows — by Cadence and Synopsys for A14, with Siemens EDA's flow coming later.

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

"Today, both Cadence and Synopsys have successfully completed full design flow certification for the Cortex-A725," the spokesman said. "This program tests the full chain of implementation and sign-off EDA tools with TSMC foundation IP. We expect it to benefit the entire A14 design community. This table summarizes A14 v0.9 certification for our three major EDA partners, Cadence, Synopsys, and Siemens, across automatic placement and routing, library characterization, timing, power, EM/IR, and other sign-off flows. Siemens is targeting Q4 for transistor-level EM/IR certification. So the A14 v0.9 design ecosystem and the PDK are ready for design-in.

Electromigration and voltage-drop analysis (EMIR)

Layout-versus-schematic checks and layout parasitic extraction (LVS/LPE)

Resistance and capacitance extraction (RC)

As of October 1, Cadence, Synopsys, and Siemens EDA all had A14 v0.9-certified tools for design rule checks, dummy fill, layout-versus-schematic verification and layout parasitic extraction (LVS/LPE), resistance and capacitance extraction, and circuit simulation. Cadence and Synopsys also had certified tools for automatic placement and routing, library characterization, timing and power analysis, custom circuit design, and electromigration and voltage-drop (EM/IR) analysis at both the design and transistor levels.

Synopsys alone had certified transistor-level timing analysis, which gave it coverage across all 13 categories versus 12 for Cadence and five for Siemens EDA. Cadence and Synopsys also completed full-flow certification on an Arm Cortex-A725 design with TSMC foundation IP, from synthesis and physical implementation through electrical, physical, and thermal sign-off. Siemens EDA's transistor-level EM/IR certification is in progress and is due in Q4 2026.

FutureMain Sets Its Sights on Nasdaq, Bringing Decades of Industrial Expertise Into the AI Era

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The South Korean industrial AI company combines 38 years of engineering expertise with explainable artificial intelligence, targeting the next generation of intelligent manufacturing.

SUWON, South Korea, Nov. 26, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- FutureMain, a South Korean industrial artificial intelligence company specializing in machinery diagnostics and predictive and prescriptive maintenance, is pursuing a Nasdaq listing as part of its strategy to expand into global manufacturing markets.

On September 11, 2026, FutureMain announced that it had entered into a definitive business combination agreement with ChampionsGate Acquisition Corp., a Nasdaq-listed special purpose acquisition company (SPAC).

Upon completion of the proposed transaction, the combined company is expected to be listed on Nasdaq. The parties anticipate closing the transaction in 2027, subject to regulatory and shareholder approvals and other customary closing conditions.

For FutureMain, the proposed listing represents more than an opportunity to raise its international profile. It is a strategic step toward building the scale, operational capacity and global service infrastructure required to serve major industrial customers.

Deploying new technologies across large manufacturing facilities and mission-critical production systems requires more than technical performance. Global manufacturers also demand long-term business continuity, dependable delivery capabilities and confidence in their technology partners.

By pursuing access to the U.S. capital markets, FutureMain aims to strengthen its international business foundation and accelerate the adoption of its industrial AI technologies.

The ambition is substantial for a company that began with just three people and had approximately 24 employees in early 2026.

Yet the company's vision is rooted in a problem that manufacturers around the world have faced for generations: understanding why machines fail â and preventing those failures before they disrupt operations.

When a major manufacturing facility unexpectedly shuts down, the consequences can extend far beyond the factory floor.

Automotive production lines, pharmaceutical plants, power generation facilities and shipyards all depend on complex machinery operating reliably, often around the clock.

And before many mechanical failures occur, equipment begins to exhibit subtle changes.

A vibration too faint for the human ear to detect. A temperature variation that is difficult to observe directly. An unusual pattern hidden within operational data.

For decades, experienced maintenance engineers have relied on specialized knowledge and practical experience to interpret these signals.

But what if artificial intelligence could learn to apply the diagnostic expertise accumulated by engineers over generations?

What if industrial equipment could be monitored not simply to detect abnormal conditions, but to identify emerging risks, explain their underlying causes and recommend appropriate maintenance actions?

That is the future FutureMain is working to create.

Turning 38 Years of Engineering Knowledge Into Industrial AI

The global artificial intelligence industry is advancing rapidly, with companies competing to develop increasingly capable models and applications.

Yet applying AI effectively in real industrial environments remains a fundamentally different challenge.

A factory cannot be fully understood through information learned from the internet alone.

Similar vibration patterns may indicate entirely different mechanical problems depending on equipment design, operating conditions and the physical mechanisms involved.

For plant operators, an alarm indicating that something is abnormal is rarely sufficient to justify stopping a critical production line.

What matters is understanding not only what is wrong, but why it is happening and what should be done next.

FutureMain developed its flagship ExRBM platform to address precisely this challenge.

Founded in 2014, the company has incorporated 38 years of accumulated machinery diagnostic engineering expertise from power plants, industrial facilities and manufacturing environments into its AI-based technologies.

ExRBM combines expert knowledge of mechanical failure mechanisms with machine learning, deep learning and actual equipment data.

Rather than relying solely on statistical anomaly detection, the platform is designed to identify specific failure mechanisms, explain the evidence behind its assessments and recommend appropriate responses.

According to company disclosures, ExRBM can automatically identify more than 85 machinery failure mechanisms, including bearing damage and shaft misalignment.

In 100 tests conducted by the Korea Testing Laboratory (KTL), the technology recorded a reported automatic diagnostic accuracy of 98.3% under the specified test conditions.

FutureMain also reports 25 patents and international recognition, including a gold medal at the International Exhibition of Inventions Geneva.

The company believes its most important advantage, however, cannot be measured by the speed at which an algorithm is developed.

It lies in the depth of engineering knowledge embedded within that algorithm.

Decades of experience analyzing normal equipment behavior, abnormal operating conditions and mechanical failures have produced a specialized body of knowledge that is difficult to replicate through software development alone.

By transforming this expertise into AI-driven diagnostic capabilities, FutureMain aims to make advanced industrial knowledge more accessible, scalable and actionable.

Proven Across Industries, From Battery Manufacturing to Shipbuilding

Industrial AI must ultimately demonstrate its value where it matters most: in operating manufacturing facilities.

FutureMain's technologies have been deployed across more than 10 industrial sectors, including battery manufacturing, pharmaceuticals and biotechnology, power generation, energy, shipbuilding and defense.

Its customer references include LG Energy Solution, Hanwha Ocean and Handok.

According to the company's 2026 investor presentation, ExRBM recorded 25 deployments over the preceding three years, while FutureMain's total customer base exceeded 48 companies.

Several customer relationships have also progressed beyond initial deployments.

At Handok's Eumseong pharmaceutical manufacturing facility, applications have expanded from equipment predictive maintenance into the integration of production-process data and quality anomaly detection across granulation, coating and tablet-compression processes.

At Hanwha Ocean, machinery diagnostic applications have been extended to additional equipment following earlier system installations.

FutureMain's collaboration with LG Energy Solution is also moving toward broader applications across domestic and overseas manufacturing operations.

The company is expanding into robotics as well.

In 2026, FutureMain has been carrying out a large-scale predictive maintenance implementation project involving welding robots and traversing equipment at HD Hyundai Construction Equipment's Ulsan facility.

These developments reflect the company's transition from solving individual machinery problems toward building an industrial AI platform applicable across different equipment types, production processes and manufacturing sectors.

International expansion is another area of focus.

FutureMain reports that it has completed a proof-of-concept evaluation at a Saudi Aramco facility and is extending its business network across the Middle East, Southeast Asia and Japan.

From its origins in Suwon, South Korea, the company is positioning its industrial AI technology for a broader role in global manufacturing.

ExRBM 2.0: Moving From Monitoring to Explainable Intelligence

In November 2026, FutureMain introduced ExRBM 2.0 at an AIoT technology presentation held at COEX in Seoul.

The next-generation platform represents an evolution from conventional equipment monitoring toward AI-assisted diagnosis and maintenance decision-making.

Traditional monitoring systems typically generate alerts when vibration, temperature or other operating parameters exceed predefined thresholds.

ExRBM 2.0 takes a more advanced approach.

By analyzing collected equipment data, the platform is designed to identify potential faults, explain their underlying causes and recommend appropriate maintenance actions.

A central feature is explainable artificial intelligence, or XAI, which allows users to examine the reasoning and evidence supporting an AI-generated diagnosis.

This distinction is particularly important in industrial environments.

When a critical machine is at risk of failure, engineers cannot always act on an unexplained warning.

They need to understand the nature of the suspected defect, the relevant frequency characteristics, the location or timing of abnormal signals and the maintenance actions that may be required.

By providing this context, ExRBM 2.0 aims to support more informed and confident decision-making.

FutureMain has also presented capabilities connecting machinery diagnostics with remaining useful life (RUL) estimation, maintenance work orders and automated diagnostic reporting.

RUL estimation is applicable depending on equipment characteristics and the availability and quality of relevant data.

In addition, the company has introduced the concept of an AI assistant based on a small language model (sLLM), enabling users to interact with machinery information and maintenance records through natural-language queries.

Instead of manually navigating multiple diagnostic screens, an engineer could ask:

"What problem has been detected in this machine?"

"Which component should be inspected first?"

"How should we determine the appropriate maintenance timing?"

The objective is not to replace experienced engineers.

It is to preserve, transfer and extend their specialized knowledge, allowing more industrial professionals to benefit from advanced diagnostic expertise.

Beyond Predictive Maintenance: AI That Understands the Entire Factory

FutureMain's long-term strategy extends well beyond machinery diagnostics.

The company is pursuing a manufacturing-focused vertical AI platform that connects equipment health, production processes, quality management and robotics.

Modern manufacturing facilities generate enormous volumes of data, including machinery vibration, electrical current, temperature, process pressure, equipment load and quality inspection results.

When these data sources are analyzed independently, manufacturers may identify isolated problems without understanding their broader operational relationships.

FutureMain sees an opportunity to connect them.

By integrating equipment-condition data with production and quality information, industrial AI could move beyond detecting individual machinery faults to understanding more complex interactions across manufacturing operations.

The company's longer-term vision includes industrial AI agents capable of interpreting factory-wide conditions, analyzing operational risks and recommending appropriate responses.

Robotics represents another potential growth area.

As industrial robots become more widely deployed, technologies capable of diagnosing their mechanical condition and identifying emerging failure patterns may become increasingly important.

FutureMain is conducting research into technologies that learn normal and abnormal robotic operating patterns, on-device AI and Physical AI applications tailored to industrial environments.

Some of these initiatives remain in research and development.

Nevertheless, the company's strategic direction is clear: to build on its established machinery diagnostic expertise and expand toward AI systems that understand manufacturing processes, product quality and the increasingly interconnected relationship between humans and robots.

The ultimate purpose of industrial AI is not defined solely by the complexity of its algorithms or the number of patents behind it.

Its value can be found in more fundamental outcomes.

Helping prevent unexpected machinery shutdowns.

Enabling more informed decisions where experienced specialists are in short supply.

And helping manufacturers produce the goods that society depends on with greater reliability.

While much of the world's attention is focused on the latest advances in generative AI, FutureMain believes the physical signals produced by industrial machinery remain an essential source of intelligence.

Behind those signals are decades of engineering knowledge and practical experience.

Shine Lee, FutureMain's Chief Operating Officer and a founding member who joined the company when it consisted of just three people, believes that this foundation will become increasingly important as the AI market matures.

"Today, countless AI-powered products and services are being created, only to disappear as quickly as they emerge," Lee said. "AI services without deep technological foundations can easily be replaced by similar offerings. In an increasingly crowded AI market, what truly matters is building AI on technologies and expertise that cannot be easily replicated."

Lee emphasized the importance of connecting artificial intelligence with the physical realities of manufacturing.

"Understanding real industrial equipment requires going directly into factories, examining machinery, installing sensors and collecting reliable physical data," she said. "The ability to acquire that data and transform it into meaningful intelligence for AI-driven decision-making is what FutureMain does best. It is also the foundation of our long-term competitive advantage."

Looking ahead, Lee described a vision that extends beyond traditional engineering AI.

"Our ambition is to evolve into a Hyperconnected Autonomous AI company â one where data from across the factory is seamlessly interconnected, enabling manufacturing operations to pursue optimal efficiency and continuous self-improvement."

This vision reflects FutureMain's belief that the next generation of industrial AI will be defined not simply by more powerful models, but by deeper integration with the physical world.

Building the Next Generation of Industrial Intelligence

FutureMain's journey, supported by 38 years of accumulated engineering expertise and a team of approximately 24 employees in early 2026, is moving beyond South Korea toward new opportunities in global manufacturing.

The proposed Nasdaq listing could represent an important milestone in that journey.

Yet the company's larger ambition is not defined by a stock market valuation.

It is rooted in the belief that AI capable of understanding real industrial environments can contribute to safer workplaces, more productive factories and a more resilient manufacturing ecosystem.

Around the world, millions of machines continue to operate inside factories, power plants, shipyards and industrial facilities.

Each generates signals that reveal something about its condition.

FutureMain is working to transform those signals into actionable intelligence â connecting decades of engineering expertise with the capabilities of modern AI.

The future of manufacturing may depend not only on making machines more intelligent, but on giving artificial intelligence a deeper understanding of the physical world.

And for FutureMain, that future is already taking shape on the factory floor.

Linkind Brings Holiday Cheer to Bowie's Crossfire Worship Center

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The community-focused project illuminated a local gathering place ahead of the Christmas season, demonstrating how smart lighting can help bring people together.

BOWIE, Md., Nov. 26, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- This holiday season, Linkind, a smart lighting brand specializing in connected lighting solutions, donated and installed permanent outdoor lights at Crossfire Worship Center in Bowie, Maryland.

Following installation, Linkind hosted a special lighting reveal at the church on Friday evening, bringing together members of the congregation and local community. Families counted down together and cheered as Linkind's holiday light show began. When the carolers started singing, bands of color moved across the church walls in time with the music. Neighbors joined in and shared warm drinks beneath the lights.

Christmas services bring families to Crossfire Worship Center during the holidays. In the months that follow, the same lights can welcome neighbors back for family gatherings and community events.

As part of its holiday community initiative, Linkind provided Crossfire Worship Center with a complimentary outdoor lighting installation, helping transform the church's exterior into a welcoming illuminated space for its congregation and surrounding community. The lights are permanently mounted along the edge of the church roof, so the church can change its display each holiday without taking them down and rehanging them.

The installation features Linkind's Smart Permanent Outdoor Lights ColorBlend System. Its dual-lens optical design casts overlapping bands of color on the church walls. The system offers 16 million colors, more than 120 preset lighting effects, and AI-assisted customization through the AiDot app. Church staff can use the app to switch the same fixtures from Christmas red and green to steady warm white above the entrance for an evening gathering.

"We're grateful for the opportunity to support Crossfire Worship Center by donating lights for this holiday display," said a spokesperson at Linkind. "We wanted to give something back to the Bowie community. We hope the finished display will be something the congregation and wider community can enjoy throughout the holiday season, and for years to come."

If you are interested in Linkind's products, you can find them here:

Linkind Smart Permanent Outdoor Lights ColorBlend | Dual Optics

Linkind is a smart lighting brand offering solutions for home, outdoor, and entertainment spaces. Its portfolio includes smart bulbs, solar and landscape lighting, permanent outdoor lights, and immersive TV and gaming lighting.

Built on the belief that lighting should do more than illuminate, Linkind combines thoughtful design with intuitive controls and intelligent automation to make advanced lighting simple, reliable, and accessible. Its products bring everyday comfort, outdoor ambiance, and personalized entertainment experiences to homes worldwide.

Serving millions of users globally, Linkind is operated by AiDot Technology Inc., which designs, sells, and supports its products.

The Character․AI mess underlines the importance of Apple’s approach to AI privacy

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

With most AI chatbots using your interactions as training data, we’ve previously cautioned against sharing sensitive personal details with them, highlighting the much stronger privacy protections offered by Apple. If you ever needed evidence of the importance of this, the Character․ai mess provides it.

The company recently filed for bankruptcy and is now offering all of its assets for sale, including the contents of user chat sessions. Some 42 state attorneys general are trying to block this, but it’s unclear whether they will succeed …

Character․AI had long come under fire for its extremely irresponsible attitude to privacy and safety. Last summer, it was accused of harvesting mental health data from underage users, and in May of this year it faced a lawsuit after its chatbots were found to be pretending to be licensed doctors. The company filed for bankruptcy last month.

A group of 42 attorneys general are trying to block the chatbot company from auctioning off users’ chat histories and other sensitive information […] “Nothing in the agreement restricts what the buyer may do with the data after closing, including selling it,” the AGs say in a court filing […]

“Character․AI’s plan to auction off its platform, including the IP addresses and private chats histories of its users — many of them children — with zero protection for those users is appalling,” California Attorney General Rob Bonta said in a statement. “If this sale goes through, users’ sensitive data would go into the hands of a buyer even though Character․AI promised consumers that their data would not be sold.”

The chat histories reportedly include conversations in which AI characters drew both adults and children into explicit sexual conversations.

Siri AI interactions are handled on-device wherever possible. Where this isn’t possible, Private Cloud Compute (PCC) servers are used, with guarantees that no data is stored or linked to your Apple account. Apple has designed the system in such a way that its privacy claims can be independently verified by security researchers.

When a user makes a request, Apple Intelligence analyzes whether it can be processed on device. For more complex requests, it can draw on Private Cloud Compute, which extends the privacy and security of your iPhone into the cloud. With Private Cloud Compute, only the data that is relevant to your request is processed on Apple silicon servers, before being removed.

The data being processed in Private Cloud Compute is not stored or made accessible to Apple. It is processed only to fulfill your request, after which point the results are returned securely to your device and are not retained by Private Cloud Compute. Independent privacy and security researchers can inspect the code that runs on Apple silicon servers that enable Private Cloud Compute to verify this privacy promise at any time.

Apple also recently introduced a safe way to ask an AI questions about your health. Data sources used are limited to the same platforms used by healthcare professionals, with links to peer-reviewed clinical citations. Apple guarantees that your chat sessions are not used for AI training with no retention of either questions or answers.

You need a winched vehicle to steal a GTA 6 ATM, and you need to know how to escape with one

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

You can start stealing GTA 6 ATMs from about midway through Chapter 3 and it'll net you some serious cash, but it's a tricky job to carry out. Technically, you can steal ATMs far earlier with a winched vehicle, but don't try it until you've picked up the dye pack jammer - it's not worth it.Since there are a few nuances to ripping ATMs out of the wall in GTA 6, including actually getting the cash out of them, I'll explain how to steal an ATM and how to get a winch in GTA 6 below, so that you can carry out these jobs like a pro.

After completing the Credit Check mission in Chapter 3, you'll have possession of a GTA 6 Dye Pack Jammer, and only then should you consider stealing ATMs out of the wall. Do it beforehand, and you'll find the cash explodes, potentially destroying your vehicle, your clothes - and the cash itself. At the right time, though, stealing ATMs can be one of the best ways to make money fast in GTA 6.

Once you've got the dye pack jammer, you can freely start nabbing ATMs with a winched vehicle, but it's only those wall-mounted to the outside of buildings. If you've also completed the Yasmani's Jewelery side quests, he'll highlight three specific locations for you where your takings will be high, but you'll only have one in-game day to grab these ones. So, whether you're doing it for Yasmani or for yourself, here's how to steal a GTA 6 ATM:

To do any of this, you need a winch. To get a winch, you need to take a suitable GTA 6 car, preferably some form of pick-up truck or large off-roader, to a mod shop. You can also steal a tow truck or other vehicle that's already got a winch, but it's probably easier to just buy one than to hunt around.

If you're planning on doing this often, it's probably also worth registering your favorite truck to yourself and putting a winch on that, so that you can call it up whenever you want to hit a few ATMs. If you aren't sure how to do this, take a look at our separate guide to vehicle ownership in GTA 6.

Meanwhile, if you're just struggling to legitimately use a GTA 6 ATM, Rockstar didn't make it easy, so we've got a standalone guide for that.

India Targets at Least 10 Million AI Workers Through National AI Talent Mission, WAVES Film Bazaar Panel Hears

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

India plans to bring at least 10 million people into its AI workforce through the National AI Talent Mission, recently launched by Prime Minister Narendra Modi, according to Lt. Col. Vikram Singh Bhati, director of the National Council for Vocational Education and Training (NCVET).

Bhati was speaking at “AI and the Future of Creative Skills: Transforming India’s Media & Entertainment Workforce,” a panel at WAVES Film Bazaar, the market held alongside the 57th International Film Festival of India (IFFI) in Goa. Moderated by voice-over artist and actor Vijay Vikram Singh, the session also featured Amit Meena, director at the Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship; K. Satish Nambudiripad, former director general of public broadcaster Doordarshan and chairperson of the Centre for Creative Economy and Public Policy at the National Institute of Technology Calicut; and Biren Ghose, founder and CEO of Astra Studios. Related Stories Marrakech's Atlas Workshops Takes Victory Lap as Industry Event Celebrates Cannes, Venice Triumphs ‘Paper Tiger’ Director James Gray Calls Out ‘Dumb, Ethically, Morally Bankrupt’ Festival Competitions and Quips That He’s Giving Up on Cannes: ‘I’ve Learned My Lesson’

“It started with the ministry launching a scheme called SOAR (Skilling for AI Readiness),” Bhati said of the government’s push to prepare workers for AI. He also called on companies to take part, saying “the industry should start absorbing people in the apprenticeship model” to help existing workers and new entrants adapt.

AI is changing the production chain, from conceptualization to release, Meena said. “It is democratizing the whole process,” he said, stressing the need to reskill existing workers.

Nambudiripad warned that AI tools “tend to homogenize,” and that “India’s folklore, regional languages and cultural nuances could be flattened” if training did not account for the country’s diversity. “You ask five people to write a concept note… if you are going through AI, every one of them will write the same,” he said. He called for technical institutions, film schools and industry to work together, and for skilling to reach India’s smaller tier-two and tier-three cities.

Creative skills are shifting “from execution to imagination,” Ghose said, and from software proficiency to technology orchestration. “In the past we were wondering when the tools would catch up with us, and now we’re wondering when we would catch up with the tools,” he said. He also called for creative technology skills to be recognized in sectors beyond film and gaming.

“No technology has ever invented anything,” Ghose said. “Everything that got invented has been invented by the ideas of man.”

The final trailer for Avengers: Doomsday confirmed Steve Rogers's choice to go back in time to live with Peggy Carter in Avengers: Endgame kickstarts the plot of the new movie.

But, there's one timeline question I can't figure out: what does that mean for Old Steve Rogers? At the end of Endgame, Sam Wilson is given the shield by an elderly Steve, who grew old in the past with Peggy. So, if he actually had to return to the present to help fight Doctor Doom, shouldn't the old Steve have already lived through Doomsday?

It's a debate Marvel fans are having in the wake of the trailer, and nobody seems to agree.

Sooooo Do we think this Steve went through the events of Doomsday or?November 22, 2026

"I keep going back and forth about this but probably? The only way for Doomsday Steve to have any stakes is if it's the same one, rather than a new branch variant created by Doom's disruption," thinks one Marvel fan.

We don't have long to find out what's actually going on. Doomsday arrives this December 18, and it pits Earth's Mightiest Heroes against Robert Downey Jr.'s Doctor Doom.

What channel is Hearts v Monaco? TV, live stream and highlights for Conference League clash

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This article contains affiliate links. We may earn a small commission on items purchased through this article, but that does not affect our editorial judgement.

Hearts will attempt to claim a major European scalp when they host French giants Monaco in the Uefa Conference League on Thursday evening.

Wouter Vrancken’s side go into the match as underdogs but are in need of a positive result after collecting just one point from their opening three league phase fixtures to sit 32nd in the Conference League table.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

A 1-1 draw against Norwegian side Nordsjælland at Tynecastle was followed by away defeats at Trabzonspor (2-0) and FC Thun (3-1) to leave the Tynecastle club facing an uphill task to qualify for the knockout stages.

Teams typically need seven or eight points from their six league phase matches to finish inside the top 24 and reach the knockout play-off round. Eight points virtually guarantees progression, while seven points usually puts a club right on the cut-off line where tie-breakers decide who advances.

Hearts know this only too well, having failed to progress on goals scored after collecting seven points to finish 25th during their 2024-25 Conference League campaign.

Monaco arrive in Edinburgh fourth in the table with a maximum haul of nine points following victories over CSKA Sofia, Nordsjælland and Freiburg. Their domestic form has been less impressive of late with Filipe Luís’ side currently on a five-game winless run on Ligue 1, where they are eighth in the standings.

The Conference League match between Hearts and Monaco takes place at Tynecastle Park, Edinburgh, on Thursday, November 26, 2026. Kick-off is 5.45pm.

The match will be shown live on TNT Sports 3 with coverage starting at 5pm.

Fans can stream the match live via HBO Max or Amazon Prime Video with a TNT Sports subscription.

Free highlights will be posted on the TNT Sports Football YouTube channel shortly after full-time.

Hearts welcome midfielder Blair Spittal back into their Europa League squad following his recovery from an ankle injury. Malachi Fagan-Walcott drops out with a groin problem. Two players who featured in the 8-0 win over Dundee on Saturday, Eduardo Ageu and Leart Kabashi, cannot play as they are not registered in the Europa League squad list.

Midfielder Tomas Magnusson is still completing concussion protocol before he can be declared available. Defender Craig Halkett and the versatile Oisin McEntee were back on the Riccarton training pitch on Wednesday, although they will not be back until next month. Winger Calvin Miller has also undergone surgery on his knee and will be out for between six weeks and two months.

Stephen Kingsley (hamstring), Harry Milne (knee) and Tom Renaud (ankle) are also unavailable.

Monaco are without five players. Goalkeeper Philippe Kohn, defender Mohammed Salisu, midfielders Stanis Idumbo and Aleksandr Golovin, plus forward Paris Brunner were all left in the Principality for treatment.

An all-German officiating team will take charge match. Daniel Schlager is the on-field referee and will be supported on the touchlines by assistant referees Sven Waschitzki and Tobias Fritsch. Robin Braun has been appointed as the fourth official. In the video operations room, Benjamin Cortus will serve as the Video Assistant Referee (VAR), alongside Nicolas Winter as the Assistant VAR (AVAR).

5 of the Greatest Sci-Fi Shows for ‘Dungeon Crawler Carl’ Fans, Ranked

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Waves of new fans are picking up the Dungeon Crawler Carl series every day, which means waves of people with the dreaded "book hangover" looking for their next fix after A Parade of Horribles. Dungeon Crawler Carl is a fantasy story at its heart, though it uses sci-fi elements and aesthetics just as much as classic fantasy tropes. If you find yourself wanting something similar to pick up after DCC, a lot of science fiction novels will miss the mark. However, sci-fi TV shows tend to have a similar blend of humor, action, and heartfelt character work. These TV shows may also be strong indications of how the upcoming DCC TV adaptation will work, especially when it comes to shows that have been adapted from books.

It's worth noting that the Dungeon Crawler Carl TV series will be produced by Fuzzy Door — the production company of Seth MacFarlane. MacFarlane has already produced a live-action sci-fi series called The Orville, which is a parody of Star Trek and other shows of its kind. Watching that might be a bit of research ahead of the DCC premiere. Beyond that, the five shows below are the most likely to appeal to Dungeon Crawler Carl fans after finishing all eight books.

If your favorite part of Dungeon Crawler Carl was the irreverent humor, Futurama should probably be your next stop. The animated series takes a satirical approach to high-tech sci-fi, though as the series goes on it delves surprisingly deep into its own world-building. It was created by Matt Groening, co-creator of The Simpsons, and it follows a young man who was cryogenically frozen and transported to a distant future. He lands a job as a courier working for his own descendant, and his travels around the planet and the galaxy lead to all kinds of hijinks.

Like DCC, Futurama has some genuinely emotional moments. It manages to dig deep into the pathos of its characters despite its silly tone, and it deals with some complex social issues as well. The show is streaming now on Hulu and it's definitely worth a try for anyone who laughed at Princess Donut's quips.

Switching gears, few people describe Apple TV's Foundation as silly or irreverent, but the massive, chaotic scale of its story fits well with Dungeon Crawler Carl. The series is based on Isaac Asimov's Foundation series, though it takes quite a few liberties in the adaptation process. The series is about the collapse of a galaxy-spanning empire, and humanity's attempts to salvage what it can as the dark age encroaches. There are three seasons of Foundation streaming now on Apple TV, with a fourth expected soon.

Rick and Morty is a natural choice for Dungeon Crawler Carl fans, and the two probably have quite a bit of crossover already. Like DCC author Matt Dinniman, the Rick and Morty writers hold nothing sacred, yet they take the consistency of their worldbuilding mechanics very seriously. The show touches on every sci-fi and fantasy trope in the book, and while it's funny, it also asks viewers to confront existential questions head on.

The most significant difference between these two stories is their worldview. DCC has resonated with many people because of its defiant, triumphant nature. No matter how much the dungeon throws at Carl and his friends, they fight back with righteous fury. Rick and Morty, on the other hand, has a tendency towards a jaded, macabre outlook. One is not necessarily better or more honest than the other, but that might be a fundamental barrier for fans enjoying both stories.

For all the traps and monsters in Dungeon Crawler Carl, the dungeon A.I. could still learn a thing or two about psychological torture from Lumon Industries. Apple TV's Severance is about a biotechnology corporation that asks employees to undergo a mysterious medical procedure known as "severance," which splits their psyche into two parts. The mind changes personas based on proximity to the company's headquarters, so employees do not remember anything that happens to them at work. Their alter egos — "innies" — do not know anything about their lives outside the office. This theoretically protects company secrets and frees the employees' main personas from experiencing tedious days at their desks.

There's so much we still don't know about Severance since the story is ongoing, but it's already clear that the show shares a disdain for corporate power with DCC. Both are about resisting oppression despite insurmountable odds, and in that way they are likely to have a huge crossover in fandom. Severance has two seasons on Apple TV now, with a third season now in post-production. There's no word yet on when the show will be back.

Finally, nothing has blurred the lines between a game and a real-life struggle quite like HBO's Westworld. The series introduced us to a futuristic theme park populated with life-like androids that behaved like normal people from the old west, allowing visitors to live out their fantasies as cowboys and pioneers — for better or worse. Over the course of four seasons, the show's scope steadily expanded to show us more about the park, its visitors, and eventually the world beyond. It's a particularly perfect binge for fans of Dungeon Crawler Carl book five, The Butcher's Masquerade and book seven, This Inevitable Ruin.

Sadly, Westworld can be tricky to find these days since it has been removed from HBO Max. The series was released on Blu-ray, so hard copies are available in some places. It can also be purchased digitally on PVOD platforms. As for Dungeon Crawler Carl, there are eight books available now in print, digital, and audiobook formats. The ninth book is coming soon, with no release date yet, and the TV adaptation is in the early stages as well.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

"Here We Go Again" - After Delaying For Silksong, Indie Dev Braces For A Sea Of Sorrow Clash

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There were a good few indies set for launch in September last year that ended up being delayed to escape the blast radius of Hollow Knight: Silksong. Aeternum Game Studios' metroidvania, Aeterna Lucis, was one such title, pushing its release date to 3rd December 2026 to ensure it was way out of the way of whatever Team Cherry was cooking up. Heh, well...

Yesterday, the Silksong dev announced an upcoming 'Cherry Time' special presentation, promising news and announcements from the studio, and an update on the hotly-anticipated Sea of Sorrow DLC. The showcase is tomorrow (27th November), so there's every chance that, in dodging the initial launch, Aeterna Lucis has wound up right in the way of the DLC.

This time, however, the dev is standing firm. "A lot of people have been waiting for the Queen of Light for a long time now, and we don't want to make them wait any longer," the dev wrote in a message on social media, neatly captioned "Here we go again".

It's all very jokey and light-hearted, with Aeternum even proposing a Silksong bundle if the dates do happen to clash. Still, there's room for a little cursing of fate: "With all the love in the world: is it just us, or does the universe have it in for us?"

You'll find the full statement below, followed by the post from Bluesky:

A little over a year ago, just as we were about to launch our game, you announced Silksong. We stepped aside, pushed the whole project back a year and, honestly, it was one of the best decisions we've ever made: the genre had an incredible year, and we got to enjoy Silksong as fans and polish Aeterna Lucis into the game we always dreamed it would be.

Now here we are again, just days from launch. Aeterna Lucis, our new metroidvania, comes out on December 3... and you announce Cherry Time. This time we're staying put. Not to go against you, but because a lot of people have been waiting for the Queen of Light for a long time, and we don't want to make them wait any longer.

Whatever happens on Friday, we'll be watching like any other fan. And if Sea of Sorrow ends up launching on the same day, the real winners will be metroidvania fans.

That said, we have to ask, with all the love in the world: is it just us, or does the universe have it in for us? Hahaha. And since you're about to wreck our lives all over again, the least we can do is make a bundle together.

With all our love (and a little bit of fear), The Aeternum Game Studios team

Official statement, part II. Here we go again…

While we can't imagine Sea of Sorrow will have quite the industry-shaking impact that the base game did, it sure would be unfortunate to see an indie like Aeterna Lucis caught in the line of fire again. Hey, at least the dev is able to have a laugh with it.

Marvel Announces New Spider-Man Villain For Next Appearance

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The latest villain to set their sights on taking down Spider-Man just debuted, but is this new rogue a serious threat or a kind of tribute act to the true Big Bads of yore in the Webslinger's extensive lore? That's to be determined. Right now, he's aiming to be the former, but Spider-Man would probably classify him as the latter.

Who is this new villain, and what is his deal? Well, the name says it all: "Scavenger." He has assembled a super-suit out of discarded pieces of gear from past Spider-Man villains, from the Vulture's wings to Jack O'Lantern's pumpkin headgear, and more.

Underneath it all, though, he's just a guy named Waldo Wagman (channeling some classic Marvel alliteration), and in the opening bout of his feud with Spidey, he doesn't exactly make a heavyweight name for himself. But he does live to fight another day, and surprisingly gains an advantage that could help him next time.

Warning! Spoilers ahead for Spectacular Spider-Man #6!

Spectacular Spider-Man #6 is really the Scavenger's story. It introduces him during a flashback to "a few years ago," taking advantage of Spider-Man and the Avengers' battle with giant robots controlled by Doctor Octopus to do some felony shoplifting. Waldo quickly gets more than he bargained for, though, when he stumbles across the Tinkerer's lab and "some of the deadliest weapons in New York."

Waldo lies low for a while, presumably practicing operating his new gear, and coming up with his supervillain name. The issue follows his erstwhile criminal debut as he tries to taken down an armored truck. This guy is definitely not Neil McCauley from Heat. What he is, though, is desperate to make some kind of name for himself, and that makes him extremely dangerous. As soon as Spider-Man shows up on the scene, Scavenger gleefully escalates from armed robbery to attempted murder.

"I get to be the guy who killed Spider-Man," he shouts as he aims a powerful laser at the hero. That goes about as one would expect. He fails, and gets beaten, and only manages to escape because Spidey is distracted saving someone. Leaving the Scavenger to vow to get payback against the Wallcrawler next issue. ​​​​

Spectacular Spider-Man #6 ends with the Scavenger lying around his "secret lair," which the issue also amusingly labels a "hovel, chatting with his suit's "onboard A.I.," which is named "Gourdo." (Every supersuit in the Marvel Universe has A.I. now, it is unavoidable.) Gourdo plays back a recording it made of Spider-Man talking to the cops, after the fight, about his own new suit. He says it came from his "guy in the chair," the guy who "builds all [his] tech": Peter Parker.

So, it is another classic Spider-Man set-up. The Scavenger doesn't know Peter is Spider-Man, but he knows who Peter is, and he wants him to build better tech for him like he does for Spidey. That is going to lead to another big confrontation between the two, which is going to help determine whether this new Spider-Man villain has the chops, or if he's little more than a jobber.

Spectacular Spider-Man #6 is available now from Marvel Comics.

What do you think of the Scavenger's debut, Spider-fans?

Nick ran his first marathon in 2016 and became obsessed with the sport. He now has PBs of 2hr 25min for the marathon and 15min 30sec for 5K. Nick is also a qualified Run Leader in the UK.

Nick is an established expert in the fitness area and along with writing for many publications, including Live Science, Expert Reviews, Wareable, Coach and Get Sweat Go, he has been quoted on The Guardian and The Independent.
